# Helper audit

Current disposition: [approved P4 layout](#p4-current-disposition-and-proposed-folder-manifest--2026-11-25).
Full semantic review: [all 690 source files](#complete-script-source-review--2026-11-24),
including the existing helper work. Earlier milestone sections below are dated
records; the complete source review takes precedence where it adds or corrects
a consumer, responsibility or retained risk.

This is the human-reviewed companion to `catalog.md`. The catalogue identifies
possible helpers structurally; this audit records what each reviewed module
actually does, who uses it, and whether it should move.

“Lifecycle relationship” means the deployment lifecycle: local qualification,
build, deployment, or runtime verification. It does not mean the chat session
lifecycle.

## Audited helpers

| Module | Harness owner | Lifecycle relationship | Evidence | Decision | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `scripts/00.chat/session-log/paths/lib.sh` | `00.chat` — session-log capability | Outside deployment lifecycle | Its declared purpose and [contract](../00.chat/session-log/paths/README.md) define canonical session identifiers and log paths. It has 23 non-test shell callers across startup, session-log maintenance, reporting, recovery, cleanup, and local merge work. | Retain in place as the canonical session-log helper library. Do not extract individual functions. | Confirmed |
| `scripts/00.chat/worktree/paths/lib.sh` | `00.chat` — worktree capability | Outside deployment lifecycle | Its declared purpose and [contract](../00.chat/worktree/paths/README.md) define deterministic worktree paths and resolve registered worktrees. It has six non-test shell callers covering session startup, worktree creation, reporting, recovery, and merge readiness. | Retain in place as the canonical worktree-path helper library. Do not extract individual functions. | Confirmed |

## Boundary observed

These are already correctly scoped capability libraries. `scripts/helpers/` is
only the cross-repository inventory and audit workspace; it is not a reason to
move working helpers away from their owning capability.

Every module below is owned by the `04.deploy` harness. The audit used each
module's declared purpose and exact non-test references. “Shared” means a
mechanism is reused while its caller keeps the stage-specific policy and proof;
it does not mean every module belongs to every lifecycle stage.

| Module | Actual role | Lifecycle relationship evidenced by callers | Decision | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

## Function-level audit: deployment-operation diagnostics

`deployment-operation-diagnostics.py` is not a single helper. It joins a
general retained-report capability to a core-server-persistence runtime binding.
The following boundary is based on source responsibility and direct callers,
not its directory name.

| Lines and functions | Actual responsibility | Consumers | Ownership decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 50–78: `runtime_contract`, `validate_runtime_diagnostic`, `validate_related_diagnostics` | Converts the executable diagnostic contract into the module's safe failure vocabulary. | Core-server-persistence runtime scripts use the validation functions; generic report validation also relies on the same contract shape. | Retain with the general diagnostics capability for now. |
| 79–297: `validate_persistence_task_failure`, `collect_persistence_task_failure`, `validate_persistence_controller_checkpoint` | Validates and collects one target's task evidence. The source hard-codes the core-server-persistence scope, its report schemas, command paths, task ARN form, account, and receipt location. | Only core-server-persistence runtime verification, administration, and rehearsal scripts. | This is a target-specific binding, not a general shared helper. It is the primary future extraction candidate. |
| 304–362: safe values, timestamps, facts, and execution validation | Rejects unsafe identifiers, evidence, timestamps, and execution vocabulary before a report is retained. | Used by the general report validator and builder. | Retain as shared report-validation mechanics. |
| 364–396: `validate_verifier_result` | Validates the persistence restore-verifier envelope. | Only core-server-persistence acceptance and restore rehearsal scripts. | Belongs with the target-specific binding above, not the general helper. |
| 398–531: `validate`, `build_report`, `write_report` | Defines, validates, and atomically writes the general retained deployment-operation report. | The package diagnostic CLI, deployment change-set scripts, and runtime evidence/rehearsal scripts. | Retain as a genuinely cross-stage shared capability. |
| 533–623: AWS and CloudFormation classification/collection | Safely classifies an existing provider failure without retaining raw provider text. | Core-server-persistence deployment transition and change-set scripts. | Retain as deployment diagnostics; it is provider-specific but not target-specific. |
| 625–698: CLI adapter | Offers the generic report capability to shell/package workflows. | The shared safe-command wrapper and the observability-collector publication workflow. | Retain with the general report capability. |

### Extraction decision

No code moves now. The evidence establishes a real split, but not a safe
mechanical one: the general `validate` and `build_report` functions currently
call the target-specific task-failure validator. Moving only the obvious
functions would leave the general report schema coupled to a hidden target
binding.

Before extraction, design one explicit boundary: the general report capability
must either stop carrying `task_failure`, or receive a caller-owned validator
for that optional evidence. Only then can the persistence-specific functions
move as one cohesive binding to the core-server-persistence runtime area.

The structural catalogue flagged these modules because they export functions.
That is only a starting signal: an export can be test support, a capability
contract, a target binding, or a shared mechanism. The decisions below are
based on their declared purpose and direct non-test callers.

| Module | Actual role | Lifecycle relationship evidenced by callers | Decision | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `20-build/core-server-persistence-composition/fixture-runtime.mjs` | Disposable compiled-fixture support: it copies, fingerprints, and cleans a test fixture. | Used by the core-server-persistence composition smoke test and its focused test programs. | Retain with the composition capability as test support. It is not a general helper and does not warrant a shared move. A future capability-folder migration may place it under `tests/`, but no move is justified now. | Confirmed |
| `20-build/runnable-program-contract/build-inputs.mjs` | Reads the declared runnable-program contract and produces approved compiler/package inputs. | Used by the runnable-program compiler and by core/entity package preparation and verification callers. | Retain in the runnable-program-contract capability as its cross-stage input boundary. It should not become a generic helper: its value is enforcing this particular declared contract. | Confirmed |
| `20-build/runnable-program-contract/compile.mjs` | Converts the declared program inputs into one bounded workspace-compiler invocation and finite diagnostics. | Used by runnable-program compilation and current package image preparation paths. | Retain beside `build-inputs.mjs` as the matching compiler adapter. The pair is a cohesive build contract, not a reason to create another shared compiler wrapper. | Confirmed |
| `40-package/build-core-server-persistence-image/runtime-contract.mjs` | Defines the core-server-persistence executable wrapper names and wrapper source. | Used only by that image capability's runtime preparation and validation. | Retain as a target-specific package binding. It must not be promoted to `shared`. | Confirmed |
| `40-package/prepare-runtime-dependencies/prepare.mjs` | Safely selects and copies a workload-bound installed dependency closure. | Used by both entity-smoke and core-server-persistence image preparation. | Retain in this package-stage capability as a shared package mechanism. Callers supply the workload binding; the helper does not choose a target. | Confirmed |
| `40-package/prepare-runtime-payload/assemble.mjs` | Safely writes validated runtime package mappings, wrappers, and a payload manifest. | Used by entity-smoke and core-server-persistence image preparation, over the lower-level workspace package implementation. | Retain in this package-stage capability as a shared payload-assembly mechanism. Do not duplicate it per image. | Confirmed |

### Boundary observed

This group supplies three different roles, despite every file containing
exports: one test-support module, two build-contract modules, one target
binding, and two reusable package mechanisms. The catalogue therefore remains
an index rather than a move list. No source code changed as part of this audit.

## Repository workspace-build audit

These seven modules total 930 lines. They are not seven copies of the same
check: five make up one reusable build foundation, while two serve distinct
repository-control capabilities. The distinction comes from their direct
callers and responsibilities, rather than their common folder.

| Module | Actual role | Lifecycle relationship evidenced by callers | Decision | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `workspace-build/declarations.mjs` | Reads the workspace graph and derives an explicitly selected package closure, compiler paths, source files, and runtime package mappings. | Used by runnable-program build inputs, image preparation, package custody, and module-structure inspection. | Retain as the shared, read-only workspace-declaration foundation. It is genuine reuse, not duplicated policy. | Confirmed |
| `workspace-build/compile.mjs` | Invokes TypeScript once for an already selected workspace closure and returns bounded diagnostics. | Used by the runnable-program compiler, the workspace command adapter, and package-image qualification. | Retain as the shared compiler mechanism. Callers continue to own which project is selected and where output is published. | Confirmed |
| `workspace-build/runtime-packages.mjs` | Plans and writes validated CommonJS shims and declared JSON assets into a runtime payload. | Used by the current core-server, core-server-persistence, and entity-smoke image paths, plus the retained platform-shell image path. | Retain as a shared package-assembly mechanism. It prevents each image capability from implementing its own filesystem safety rules. | Confirmed |
| `workspace-build/script.mjs` | Converts a bounded command-line request into a selected workspace compile. | The repository's focused runtime-test and image-build commands invoke it for many independent packages. | Retain as the public command adapter for this foundation. It is an operation wrapper, not another check. | Confirmed |
| `workspace-build/project-selection.mjs` | Derives workspace roots for the older `--project-roots` image-build route without approving imports merely because they resolve. | Used by the workspace command adapter and retained platform-shell image preparation. | Retain as a narrow compatibility adapter within workspace build. Do not extend it to new routes; future work can retire it when that image route is retired. | Confirmed narrow scope |
| `check-selection/check-inputs.mjs` | Reads supported npm check commands to report their actual source and installation inputs without running them. | Called by `scripts/repo/check-selection/workspace_context.py`. | Relocated with its existing proof to `check-selection` on 2026-11-26. Build declarations remain with the compiler; only the selector owns input projection. | Confirmed ownership mismatch |
| `workspace-build/dependencies.mjs` | Scans literal imports across workspace source and reports undeclared, unresolved, or test-only runtime dependencies. | Imported only by `scripts/repo/module-structure/check.mjs`. | Retain for now as module-structure inspection support, not as a build helper. A future bounded relocation should move it with module structure and its tests. | Confirmed ownership mismatch |

### Boundary observed

`declarations`, `compile`, `runtime-packages`, `script`, and the limited
`project-selection` adapter form a coherent shared build foundation. They are
changed only when their common build contract changes, and focused callers can
prove those changes.

`check-inputs` and `dependencies` are different. The former supports test
selection; the latter supports a broad architecture scan. Their current folder
creates a misleading impression that every `workspace-build` change is a build
change. Their eventual moves should be small owner migrations, including their
direct caller and focused tests—not a rewrite of the workspace foundation.

The import scanner is particularly relevant to verification cost: it inspects
workspace source as part of the module-structure capability. Whether it runs
on a normal change, only when architecture inputs change, or as scheduled
hygiene is a selection-policy decision to audit separately. This audit changes
neither the scanner nor its scheduling.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Its named risk is changing, deleting, or duplicating an existing diagnostic identity while retaining the same code-and-version address. | `export-catalogs.mjs` is the only production caller; its focused cases in `smoke-test.py` reject changed definition content, deletion, version replacement, and duplicate identity. | Local diagnostic-catalogue generation and verification, not product build or deployment. | Retain beside the diagnostic catalogue exporter as a narrow compatibility guard. It is not a cross-repository helper and must not move to `scripts/helpers/`. | Confirmed narrow scope |
| `scripts/repo/module-structure/check.mjs` | Implements the repository's public-index, configuration-access, module-boundary, and workspace-dependency scan. The complete scan is manually selectable; its fixture regression remains the automatic proof when its own checker or fixtures change. | Retain as one owned module-structure capability. Do not split exported fixture functions into a shared helper library or restore the complete scan to ordinary commit/merge selection. | Confirmed |

## Milestone-four design: deployment-operation diagnostics

This is a design record only. It does not move code, alter a report, change a
check, or add a compatibility wrapper.

### Current boundary and risk

`scripts/04.deploy/shared/deployment-operation-diagnostics.py` owns the
general `deploy/deployment-operation-diagnostic/v1` report: its base fields,
safe report writing, command-line record/validation route, and safe AWS and
CloudFormation failure classification. Its optional `task_failure` report
field is different: it is meaningful only for Core Server Persistence task
evidence, but the shared `validate` and `build_report` functions currently
call that target-specific validator directly.

The named risk is a future shared report change silently changing, accepting,
or rejecting Core Server Persistence task evidence—or a persistence change
needlessly forcing package and deployment-report callers through target-only
logic.

The tracked retained operation reports were inspected: none contains
`task_failure`. Current production callers also do not pass `task_failure` to
`build_report`; it exists in the general format only because the shared module
contains the target reader. This is evidence to remove the hidden dependency,
not authority to discard historical reports or task evidence.

### Direct consumer map

| Consumer group | Current use | Ownership after the designed split |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `safe-command-diagnostics.sh` and `provider-adapters/aws/ecr_publication.py` | Invoke the shared `record` command to retain a generic package failure report. | Shared report command, unchanged. |
| Core Server Persistence `create-change-set` and `transition-qualified-release` | Classify a named CloudFormation failure, then build and write a deploy report. | Shared report and CloudFormation classifier, unchanged. |
| `rehearse-isolated-database-restore` | Builds/writes a general run report; separately validates the persistence restore-verifier result. | General report calls remain shared; verifier-result reader moves to the target binding. |
| `collect-acceptance-evidence` | Validates a persistence restore-verifier result. | Target binding. |
| `rehearse-observability-readiness` | Selects a safe persistence controller checkpoint. | Target binding. |
| `rehearse-runtime-credential-lifecycle` | Selects controller checkpoints and merges bounded persistence task failures. | Target binding. |
| `run-administration` | Collects an administration task failure, then separately uses the generic runtime-diagnostic reader. | Task-failure collection moves to target binding; generic runtime-diagnostic reader stays shared. |
| `verify-candidate` | Collects a verification task failure from a bounded task-log response. | Target binding. |
| `run-migration` | Validates generic runtime and related diagnostic records only. | Shared diagnostic-record readers, unchanged. |

The current shared smoke test covers both groups. Existing focused caller proof
also includes the `verify-candidate` operation test. No caller outside the
Core Server Persistence runtime capability uses the persistence-only functions.

### Chosen interface

Use a caller-owned extension profile rather than changing or replacing the
general report schema.

The shared report capability will own only its base fields. It will accept an
explicit profile consisting of exact extension-field names and validators. Its
default profile has no extensions, so the shared command and ordinary report
callers remain unable to admit target evidence accidentally.

The Core Server Persistence binding will own a profile for the one extension:

```text
task_failure -> validate_persistence_task_failure
```

That binding will also own these target-only functions together:

- `validate_persistence_task_failure`;
- `collect_persistence_task_failure`;
- `validate_persistence_controller_checkpoint`; and
- `validate_verifier_result`.

The shared validator will no longer name `task_failure` or call any
persistence-specific function. The target binding will call the shared report
validator with its explicit profile when it needs to read a report containing
that extension. A generic report without target evidence remains valid through
the current shared public command.

This is preferable to simply deleting the field because it keeps a bounded
reader for any real Core Server Persistence report that contains task evidence,
without making every generic caller load the persistence contract. It is also
preferable to a new global diagnostics registry: the profile is a local
binding supplied at the real target boundary.

### Planned canonical home and migration order

The target binding's proposed home is:

```text
scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/
  core-server-persistence/deployment-diagnostics/
    binding.py
    tests/binding-test.py
```

This is a proposed M5 capability unit, not a created folder. It will require
approval before creating source files over the repository's 50-line limit.

M5 must proceed in this order:

1. Add the target binding and transfer the existing persistence-only fixture
   cases from the shared smoke test; do not duplicate them.
2. Add the explicit extension-profile seam to the shared report engine and
   prove its default profile rejects target-only fields.
3. Move all listed target callers together to the binding; retain the generic
   runtime-diagnostic calls at their current shared owner.
4. Transfer generic report, AWS/CloudFormation, and command-line cases to the
   shared test; transfer task-failure, controller-checkpoint, and verifier
   cases to the binding test.
5. Update the existing selector inputs and focused repository-contract proof
   to follow the two canonical owners, then remove the target-only functions
   from the shared module in the same slice.

### Proof and rollback

The focused proof set for M5 is deliberately the existing proof set divided by
responsibility, not a new suite:

- shared report/CLI and CloudFormation fixtures retain their current smoke
  coverage;
- transferred persistence fixtures prove extension admission, malformed task
  evidence rejection, bounded observation merge, controller checkpoint
  selection, and verifier-result rejection;
- `verify-candidate/operation-test.py` proves the target binding through a
  real caller; and
- the existing `deployment-operation-diagnostics-regression` proves the
  connected catalogue and declared controller inputs after the canonical paths
  are updated.

Before removal, a source search must show that no production caller imports
the old target functions. A generic report test must reject a supplied
`task_failure` without the target profile, while the target binding test must
accept only its validated form.

If the migration fails before acceptance, retain the current shared module and
do not publish a partial path move. If an accepted M5 commit needs reversal,
revert that bounded commit; do not rewrite retained reports, relax validation,
or introduce a compatibility wrapper without a concrete remaining caller.

## Milestone-five migration: Core Server Persistence deployment diagnostics

The proposed capability is now the canonical implementation at
`scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/deployment-diagnostics/`.

`binding.py` owns the four persistence-only readers: task-failure validation,
task-failure collection, controller-checkpoint selection, and restore-verifier
result validation. The six mapped target callers load that binding for these
functions. `rehearse-isolated-database-restore` and `run-administration` keep
their genuinely generic report and diagnostic-record calls in the shared
module.

The shared module now knows only the base deployment report. Its default
validator rejects `task_failure`; the target binding supplies that one field's
validator explicitly through `report_extension_validators()`. This is a local
binding, not a registry or a compatibility path.

The old mixed smoke fixture was divided without adding a selector entry:

- the retained shared test proves generic reports, CloudFormation handling,
  command-line behaviour, and wrapper dispatch;
- `deployment-diagnostics/tests/binding-test.py` proves malformed evidence
  rejection, bounded task-log merge, checkpoint selection, verifier envelopes,
  and the affected direct controllers; and
- the existing `deployment-operation-diagnostics-regression` still runs both,
  with the binding and its test declared as direct inputs.

Focused proof passed for the shared wrapper, target binding test,
`verify-candidate/operation-test.py`, and the selector's repository-contract
test. A source audit confirms no persistence-only function definition remains
in the shared module and all six mapped callers name the target binding.

The retained risk is limited: third-party or untracked historical reports with
`task_failure` must be read through this Core Server Persistence binding, not
the generic command-line validator. No tracked retained report has that field,
and no compatibility wrapper was added.

## Admission standard recorded after the first bounded migration

The diagnostics migration showed that an executable file cannot be placed or
selected safely from its filename or legacy path alone. The new harness
standard, `.agentic/01.harness/standards/script-admission.md`, records the
small decision needed before a future script is created: its role, owner and
capability, purpose, direct consumer, named risk, proof lane, reuse decision,
and why an existing capability cannot own it unchanged.

It does not create another inventory or scheduling mechanism. The existing
catalogue remains structural evidence, this audit remains the human decision
ledger, and the existing selector remains the only ordinary selection owner.
Until the owner/domain/capability folder migration reaches a relevant area,
the admission record requires a conservative existing-home decision or an
approved proposal for a new capability folder.

## Complete script-source review — 2026-11-24

This section supersedes the earlier **34 helper candidates** as the limit of
our source review, not as a new execution registry. The earlier decisions and
diagnostics migration history remain above. Where the broader review found a
missed caller or mixed responsibility, this current section takes precedence. The existing simplification plan
revision 18 owns implementation and approval.

### What was reviewed

Every one of the **690 tracked source files** under `scripts/` at commit
`33f479b12453db7f09e2c2cfbc77cecd55952145` was read in full, including metadata,
embedded shell/Python/JavaScript, fixture setup, test assertions and top-level
execution. Reviewers traced the visible imports, wrappers, catalogue entries
and relevant workflow consumers. Each file has one semantic record below;
ownership was not inferred solely from its path or name.

The fixed source suffix set was `.py .sh .mjs .cjs .js .ts .tsx .jsx`.
The 901 tracked script-area paths also include 185 Markdown files and 26 other
data/configuration files. Relevant external contracts and callers were inspected
to understand the sources, but there is no claim that every document/data file
or every product source outside `scripts/` was audited in full.

| Review group | Source files | Physical lines, including metadata and tests |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Chat, harness, repository utilities and helper generator | 150 | 39,461 |
| Retained RAG and deployment capabilities outside lifecycle/shared | 178 | 50,931 |
| Alignment, build, package, deployment and shared adapters | 195 | 29,202 |
| Generic runtime, bootstrap and Entity Smoke production/support | 98 | 18,152 |
| Core Server Persistence runtime, including its tests | 50 | 9,728 |
| Direct Entity Smoke Python test programs | 19 | 8,289 |
| **Total** | **690** | **155,763** |

The review took place only in the session-recorded November 21 worktree.
No reviewed module was imported or executed. No test suite, installation,
container, cloud request, source migration, check disablement, commit, merge or
push was performed. Documentation-only checks reconcile the exact file set,
uniqueness, physical line counts, source state and report structure.

**Assurance limit:** a full-body static review reduces known surprises; it is
not a formal proof of every branch, complete dynamic call-graph discovery,
runtime success, a security certification, or measured performance. Findings
distinguish visible source contradictions from possible runtime consequences.
Proof routes in the ledger are existing candidates to inspect and use safely;
they are not tests passed by this review.

### Main conclusion in plain English

The problem is not simply “too many files.” The recurring problems are:

1. **A small command can hide a lot of work.** Some leaf checks import complete
   test programs, compile repeatedly, or call broad inventory scans. Selection
   at the outer command is not sufficient to establish its real cost.
2. **Saved proof must say what was actually tested.** One confirmed selector
   gap binds the on/off flag for changed-file context but not its actual value.
   A partial Core run must not count as a different subset or the full suite.
3. **Some files join genuinely different jobs.** Runtime control, historical
   incident readers, provider transport, fixtures and assertions sometimes
   share one module. Conversely, many helpers are already correctly owned and
   should stay where they are.
4. **Moving files can change authority, not just imports.** Retained release
   records, controller source hashes, public-image recovery allowlists and
   Docker copy inputs depend on exact paths or bytes. Those consumers belong
   in the migration packet before the move.
5. **Some local tests are not isolated.** Examples change real checkout files,
   rename installed dependencies, use fixed ports, inherit Git/provider
   environment, or depend on an early refusal to avoid cloud commands. Serial
   execution does not make those effects harmless.
6. **There are real correctness debts.** Examples include stale template
   expectations, inconsistent build identities, incomplete failure/cleanup
   handling and insufficiently bound live observations. These are not reasons
   to delete all checks; they identify the precise behavior that needs proof.
7. **Legacy is not the same as inactive.** Staging has active scheduled routes;
   dormant RAG retains real query/build code. Shared test bodies can still pull
   those systems into current verification even when outer selection isolates
   them.

This does not mean all findings must be fixed before the current branch can
merge. The plan keeps normal selection/evidence/promotion first; retained-layer
debts are conditional on relying on or modifying their affected behavior.

### How the next executor must use this record

- Use P1–P7 in the existing plan, not the finding order as a new task queue.
- Read the current packet's rows and visible callers. Reconcile changed files
  against the recorded source head instead of repeating all 690 reads.
- Before disabling any check, record its named risk, actual current selection
  trigger, nested execution, direct consumers, retained manual/deferred route
  and evidence invalidation. This audit disables nothing.
- Before extraction/movement, inspect dynamic loaders, relative roots, embedded
  programs, protected command bodies, source-byte tables and historical readers,
  not only literal references.
- Keep live freshness separate from immutable source/history validation.
  Preserve one-submission journals, positive resource ownership and unknown
  outcome reconciliation. Do not retry a cloud mutation to obtain a green test.
- Treat “split”, “move”, “correct” and “isolate” below as proposals, not approval.
  “Retain” does not assert defect-free behavior. A test file's proof description
  is not a recommendation to run it indiscriminately.
- Where a named risk remains deferred, say so. No opaque global cleanup gate,
  replacement registry, new scheduler or speculative wrapper framework follows
  from this review.

### Terms used in the detailed evidence

A **fixture** is controlled test setup. A **receipt** is a saved machine-readable
result; **custody** binds that result to its source, image and release.
A **hash/fingerprint** identifies exact contents. **Admission** means permission
to begin an operation; **reconciliation** means observing what happened to the
original attempt instead of repeating it. **HEAD** is Git's current commit.

**AWS** is Amazon Web Services. **ECS** runs container tasks; **RDS** supplies
managed databases; **IAM** controls permissions; **ECR** stores container images;
**CloudFormation** applies infrastructure plans. **CLI** means command-line
interface; **CI** means automated integration checking. **RAG** here is the
retained retrieval/rulebook system. **SLO** means a service performance objective;
**OTLP** is the telemetry transport protocol. **SQL** is database query language;
**TLS** encrypts connections. JSON and YAML are structured data-file formats.
Technical identifiers below name the actual code; their surrounding text
explains the responsibility and risk.

### Full-review findings

Finding prefixes identify review areas, not severity: R = repository/harness,
L = retained RAG and outer deployment capabilities, D = delivery/build/package,
U = generic and Entity runtime, V = persistence runtime, ET = Entity test bodies.
Some findings overlap earlier F01–F22; they provide more source detail, not
additional independent defects or automatic checks. Every finding below is
static unless explicitly stated otherwise. The current programme, not the
suggested `P` labels in a finding, determines execution order and authority.

#### Alignment, build, package, deploy and shared mechanisms — findings

| ID | Source finding | Named risk | Bounded follow-up |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | Shared diagnostics import a target runtime contract<br>deployment-operation-diagnostics.py imports persistence vocabulary; its shell regression also invokes persistence bindings and restoration-surface fixtures. | Shared/diagnostic routing hides runtime ownership and test fan-out. | P4/P6: Record the runtime edge; extract only invariant report transport while retaining target-owned vocabulary and complete handoff fixtures. |
| D-02 | Collected CloudFormation events are not classification evidence<br>collect_cloudformation_failure fetches stack events; classifier intentionally excludes uncorrelated events and regression asserts no event facts. | Extra live observation adds latency/authority without changing the current decision. | P4/P5: At the diagnostic owner prove whether another consumer needs events, then remove unused collection or explicitly bind its evidence and freshness. |
| D-03 | Selected leaf checks can enter deferred or staging work<br>shared recovery-test imports staging controllers; runtime-preparation diagnostics include staging preparations; package custody regressions include staging workflow/payload and missing-engine cases enter real builder gates. | Catalog selection alone does not bound the transitive executed work or uphold staging exclusion. | P2/P5/P6; selected command closure: Record nested subprocess/import bodies and top-level side effects; separate reusable fixture helpers from suite execution with explicit leaf/aggregate routes. |
| D-04 | Repository inventory gates are consulted per operation with module-local caching<br>AWS inventory_covered uses lru_cache(maxsize=8); target adapters consult it per provider operation, with cached response modules in several callers. Scans can repeat across subprocesses or independently loaded module instances. Declaration coverage defaults probe_commands=True and can invoke acceptance-route inspect. | The cache's module/root scope and transitive acceptance probes complicate cost, freshness and isolation; per-operation gate calls are not proof of per-operation full scans. | P5; measure actual cache misses: Measure actual subprocess/module-instance counts before optimization. Specify immutable inventory/declaration invalidation and separate repository evidence from freshness-sensitive live probes. |
| D-05 | Build/package gates repeatedly compile and qualify unchanged inputs<br>Core smoke always payload fixture; Core builder boundary+build+Docker compile; persistence pgcheck, integration and composition then Docker compile; Entity workflow selection, appcheck, composition then Docker; payload wrapper runs six workspace regressions. | Large checks are not bounded by wrapper names; blind caching could skip authoritative full handoff. | P4/P5: Measure end-to-end compile/proof counts and reuse content-bound immutable evidence at the owning workflow while preserving container/producer and negative handoff coverage. |
| D-06 | Prerequisites, shared ports and fixed waits contradict cheap-fixture assumptions<br>Persistence composition binds OTLP4318, uses PostgreSQL/OpenSSL and fixed wait; image smoke uses39454/39455; Entity qualification fixture uses real OpenSSL despite fake Docker; collector behavior can pull Node image and waits16s for a negative flush. | Parallel collisions, undeclared tools/network, and timeouts make local budgets/isolation misleading. | P2/P5: Catalog actual tools/effects/ports and measured budgets; use scoped resources and readiness bounds; retain meaningful negative exporter duration. |
| D-07 | Dependency graph owners are not uniform<br>Core boundary follows imports with its own graph while Entity/shared workspace paths consume declaration/compiler helpers. | Moving similarly named validators together can preserve duplicated graph semantics or silently change boundaries. | P4/P6: Compare compiler-derived graph semantics against bootstrap fixture cases before consolidating; reuse existing workspace owner where behavior is equivalent. |
| D-08 | Runnable declaration parsing repeats across programs<br>runnable-program validator invokes the Node declaration reader per program and build callers repeat unchanged graph/contract checks. | Needless subprocess parsing and unclear fingerprint ownership. | P4/P5: Expose one invocation-scoped parsed declaration/graph with precise source/tool invalidation, retaining per-program decision evidence. |
| D-09 | Legacy alignment behavior includes deliberate weak/last-wins semantics<br>Generic alignment duplicates checks into a dict with last-wins behavior asserted by tests; malformed entries can be ignored; role presence is not IAM authorization; foundation finding vocabularies differ. | Treating a move as pure relocation would conceal policy changes or retain weak assertions accidentally. | P4/P6: Inventory these semantics as explicit retain/correct decisions; add fail-closed duplicate/type and finding-code coverage at the real owner when approved. |
| D-10 | Deployment regression bodies span package, deploy and runtime protocols<br>Persistence controller-regression exercises replacement, snapshot, migration, admin, restore and credentials; provider regression covers historical receipts; connected payload test crosses real assembly/reservation/change-set/task/succession boundaries. | Directory-based ownership and test splitting can omit full handoff failures or misstate runtime cost. | P4/P5/P6: Assign mixed responsibility explicitly; extract fixture transport without duplicating policies, keep a named complete-handoff integration route and focused leaves. |
| D-11 | ECR access proof duplicates error interpretation with distinct policy<br>validate-delivery-access/ecr-read-access.py parses provider failures independently of shared CLI classifier and lacks explicit subprocess timeout. | Similar implementations may diverge, while indiscriminate unification can alter fail-closed operation semantics. | P4/P6: Consolidate transport/error normalization only after a policy matrix and concrete timeout/retry/error-shape regressions; distinguish CLI retries from subprocess counts. |
| D-12 | Structural guards coexist with substring and handwritten parsers<br>Shared strict CloudFormation loader exists; ECS configuration/collector loader differ; IAM coverage parses Action indentation; workflow/target guards retain substring checks; Entity workflow-test has test-local publication-foundation predicate. | Tests can prove their own mirror rather than production policy; malformed/duplicate and semantic YAML changes may evade text guards. | P4/P6: Route structural checks through existing owners, cover production predicates, and retain strict fixed command/custody policy where intentional. |
| D-13 | File locations and source bytes participate in retained authority<br>qualify-local-public-images.sh pins reviewed SHA256 source table; persistence reservations hash controller/template/shared source; Entity repair source allowlists/flat source_paths; archived rollback receipts preserve historical contracts. | A source move/edit can invalidate retained reentry, recovery or historical acceptance even if imports still resolve. | P4/P6: Include every source/receipt/hash/Docker-copy consumer in migration manifest; define explicit historical reconciliation rather than compatibility aliases or silent hash rewrites. |
| D-14 | Persistence builder samples source revision twice and regression blesses mismatch<br>build-core-server-persistence-image/script.sh obtains git HEAD for label and again for KANBIEN_SOURCE_REVISION; qualification-route-test.py returns differing hashes and asserts both are used successfully. | One image can carry inconsistent source identities if HEAD changes between observations. | P5: Capture a single immutable revision before gates and bind label/build argument/receipt to it; change the regression to reject drift and record provenance closure. |
| D-15 | Custody trust resides in producer workflow, not receipt shape alone<br>Receipt v1/v2 validators check shapes; qualification verifies recorded input list/image/config hashes; target guards constrain exact producer input lists/order; Core records a narrower source set than persistence/Entity; guards duplicate protected shell bodies. | Arbitrary or incomplete producer declarations can look structurally valid; refactors may either weaken trust or cause rigid guard drift. | P2/P5/P6: Document producer-to-evidence trust and required input closure at one owner; test malformed/missing/substituted evidence, exact selected-check handoff and image mutation; synchronize protected producer changes deliberately. |
| D-16 | Deployment controllers share machinery but not equivalent review policy<br>Bootstrap checks exact templates/parameters and logical actions; persistence CREATE uses allowed resource types without proposed-template equality/action/logical-ID/pagination checks. Permission update lacks Replacement guard; restoration uses ID sets plus type union; retire-failed-candidate has no accepted-release read despite never-accepted claim. | A generic helper boundary could launder weaker review as equivalent authority; current gaps need explicit operational-risk decisions. | P4/P6: Create operation-specific invariant matrix and negative fixtures before consolidation: template/source identity, complete changes, ID-to-type/action/replacement, prior acceptance and retained intent. Keep transport/reservation reuse separate. |
| D-17 | Collector behavior qualification references a deleted template resource<br>behavior-test.py:78 reads Resources.ObservabilityCollectorConfiguration.Properties.Value; current persistence template instead embeds AOT_CONFIG_CONTENT at line657, and deployment smoke line366 asserts the old resource absent. | The required metric qualification statically reaches KeyError before exercising behavior, despite custody source guards passing. | P4/P5: Repair configuration extraction at the existing template/config owner; add local positive actual-template extraction and old/missing/duplicate configuration negatives, then retain real container exporter proof. |

Catalog alone lists own directory; effective invalidation requires review. | P2/P6: P2 audit real transitive inputs and P6 separate stable minimal fixture inputs from one deliberate integration handoff; do not delete all-scope ownership assertion. |

Actual cost not timed. | P5/P6: Before extraction inventory runtime loader identities, actual source closure, historical controller readers and count prepare calls. Preserve fresh mutation guard and one-submission reservations; share immutable validation only with explicit evidence identity. |

| U08 | Some internal helper effect declarations do not describe injected provider mutations<br>Entity restore-control.py metadata effects network,writes-files; cleanup_restore submits cloudformation delete-stack and prepare/execute submit create/execute via provider; caller restore-route.py owns authorization. | A path/header-only audit can misclassify dangerous helpers as read-only or safe to execute during checks. | P4/P7: Classify actual behavior and caller authority, keep operation helper under target owner, correct effects metadata when owner changes; never execute reviewed entrypoints merely to discover behavior. |

| V-09 | Persistence verification ownership crosses wrapper and real-scope fixture boundaries<br>scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/acceptance-route-rehearsal/smoke-test.sh:22; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/acceptance-route-rehearsal/smoke-test.py:166; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/acceptance-route-rehearsal/smoke-test.py:350; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/collect-acceptance-evidence/smoke-test.py:126; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/verify-candidate/smoke-test.sh:57; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/50-deploy/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/smoke-test.sh:452; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/50-deploy/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/controller-regression.py:1627; scripts/repo/check-selection/checks.yml:3291 | Medium: acceptance aggregate combines receipt/schema units, historical applicability, actual CLI fixture handoffs and observability; it copies real target scopes and executes real inventory validators. Administration/migration/task regressions are reached through verifier and 50-deploy wrappers, and runtime lifecycle/replacement/snapshot behavior is also tested in the 50-deploy controller suite. Folder-local selection would miss true owners or repeated work. Current broad acceptance inputs include these real scope dependencies; this is not proof of stale cache reuse. | P2/P5: Inventory actual selected leaves and their direct/transitive inputs before narrowing checks. Keep one coherent complete handoff aggregate, extract reusable pure fixtures only where equivalent risk coverage is retained, and measure duplicated package gates/validator inventories. Preserve the existing strong reservation/replay and lifecycle-order regressions. |
| V-10 | Administration/migration recovery is coupled to today's definition unlike verifier recovery<br>scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/run-administration/script.py:285; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/run-administration/script.py:295; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/run-migration/script.py:353; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/run-migration/script.py:379; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/task-operations.py:143; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/verify-candidate/script.py:481; scripts/04.deploy/lifecycle/60-run/targets/kanbien-core-platform/core-server-persistence/task-operation-test.py:176 | High: normal administration/migration continuation reserves against the current request before observing retained work, while explicit reconciliation validates against current outputs/task definition. An unfinished reservation can become blocked when the selected definition/deadline changes. Migration historical capture can retain a receipt but does not finish the operation journal. Verifier already has an explicit read-only original-reservation path, so recovery semantics diverge across closely related task controllers. | P4/P6; affected task recovery: Extend the existing shared task binding with bounded, read-only reconciliation of the exact original reservation and completion, independent of today's admission prerequisites. Keep new operations gated on current inputs and predecessor completion. Test old-definition/deadline recovery and ensure no resubmission or cross-operation adoption. |

#### Entity Smoke direct Python tests — findings

| ID | Source finding | Named risk | Bounded follow-up |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ET-01 | Import-time fixture work must be distinguished from guarded fixture libraries<br>source-transition-test.py:155 imports task-evidence-test.py; that file executes all cases/diagnostic/pagination assertions at module load. By contrast deployment-control, task-binding, task-route, failure-route, restart-route and acceptance-aggregate export guarded fixtures. Several direct suites intentionally execute at top level. | The base wrapper runs source-transition then task-evidence explicitly, so the evidence suite runs twice; broadly treating all test imports as fixtures or all as suite execution misstates cost. | P5/P6: Extract reusable evidence fixture construction into an import-safe owner or guard suite execution, retaining the explicit leaf and full-handoff route; measure module loading separately. |
| ET-02 | Historical custody is a first-class invariant in the test bodies<br>Source-transition/image-repair fixtures pin exact historical packet/gate hashes; resource control independently constructs v1 reservation bytes; task and restore tests permit expired provider records only with immutable original admissions/completions and explicit historical attribution. | A relocation or source hash change can invalidate retained recovery even if current imports work; a blanket fresh-check policy could accidentally replay operations. | P4/P6: Carry historical file/byte/source closure into migration inventory and require unchanged archive reuse, changed-custody rejection and no-new-effect tests for every moved owner. |
| ET-03 | Controlled full handoff is substantial and distinct from live qualification<br>Task, credential and restore routes run actual reservation, gate, admission, collector, completion and archive producers against fake provider/source/custody prerequisites. Aggregate fixtures explicitly mock primitive validators and only prove connection rules. | Removing nested tests as duplicate or promoting simulated evidence to live acceptance would weaken the authoritative handoff. | P4/P5/P6: Separate fixture transport, primitive protocol checks and named connected integration proofs; preserve exact named negative cases and label each evidence class honestly. |
| ET-04 | Existing tests already encode narrow cache and freshness boundaries<br>task-binding-test.py asserts one source snapshot per inspection, deep-copy observation reuse inside scope, and no caching for tasks/services/logs/simulation/mutation. task-route-test.py asserts one source per preparation, a fresh pre-reservation check and no fallible reads after reservation. Historical receipts deliberately do not claim current readiness. | Global caches or broad rechecking can respectively stale authority or block/replay historical operations. | P2/P5: Use these existing owner-level contracts for evidence reuse; add measured invocation counts without weakening fresh mutation-boundary checks or original-admission semantics. |
| ET-05 | Route labels conceal broad but meaningful execution graphs<br>Composition base includes acceptance route/control archives and real Git histories. task-route main runs successor reuse, full requalification, two recovery-failure branches, provider matrix and baseline, including restore-route.run. credential route includes snapshot/restore/cleanup/final archives. restore-route direct main executes credential-route via runpy. | Published leaf budgets and isolation may describe less work than is actually run; splitting by filename can omit dependent acceptance proof. | P2/P5: Map exact executable graph and measure each branch; retain complete handoffs explicitly, introduce focused routes only with named risk coverage and coherent fingerprint invalidation. |
| ET-06 | Disposable Git fixtures use inconsistent environment isolation<br>acceptance-route-test.py recovery_applicability_protocols uses cwd=temp but inherits ambient GIT_* and hooks/global/system configuration; source_boundary_protocols in the same file strips GIT_*, disables global/system config and hooks. | Ambient GIT_DIR/GIT_WORK_TREE or hook configuration can redirect supposed fixture writes or run unrelated hooks, invalidating isolation and repeatability. | P2/P4/P6: Reuse one existing safe fixture Git boundary for all disposable history operations; test hostile Git environment/config and confine writes before executing these suites. |
| ET-07 | Offline CLI sentinel tests are not themselves time-bounded<br>Infrastructure, task, failure and restart route fixtures invoke real shell/Python wrappers through subprocess.run without timeout; acceptance-control and acceptance-route use20s/15s bounds for comparable wrapper tests. | A regression in offline parsing/imports can hang an otherwise controlled local check and exceed declared development feedback budgets. | P2/P5: Reuse the existing bounded subprocess pattern at the affected fixture, preserving provider tripwires and explicit timeout outcomes; no new general wrapper layer is required. |
| ET-08 | Shared fixture globals are restored inconsistently on failure<br>failure-route-test.scenario restores patched module globals after yield rather than in finally; task-route.run and infrastructure top-level suites restore many manual monkeypatches only after successful bodies; restore-route.run has end-of-function override restoration. | An assertion raised inside a reused fixture can leave module state redirected to deleted temporary roots and contaminate subsequent in-process tests or mask the original failure. | P4/P6: Use context-managed patches/ExitStack or try/finally at fixture owners; verify a deliberate mid-scenario failure leaves all imported consumers and roots unchanged. |

### Complete per-file source ledger

All rows below have status **source-reviewed**, not **tests passed**. The boundary column describes actual source responsibility, not a claim about automatic selection. The visible caller/proof list is source evidence, not exhaustive discovery of generated or external consumers. A missing automatic test caller is recorded where found; no runner is invented to fill it. Named risks include the proposed decision and linked findings; exact triggers/invalidation are finalized in the approved change packet before altering selection.

The six groups partition the inventory for navigation only. Semantic owners and roles were established by reading the source and its consumers. Expand only the group needed for the current packet.

### Non-obvious consumers and hold points

| Boundary | Actual consumer / required handling before its bounded move |
| --- | --- |
| Placement authority | Current standard/validator has no legacy home and does not express E01's cohesive operation/role layout. Obtain exact layout approval, then reconcile that existing owner and its focused fixtures before P6. No 76 tiny component folders, blanket allowances or second placement standard. |
| Public invocation | Update package.json, workflow command bodies, active agent instructions, metadata, target declarations, selector argv/inputs and diagnostic references together. Keep public npm aliases/arguments/results and GitHub workflow root; old internal paths need per-packet compatibility review. |
| Dynamic roots | Check ROOT/parents, Path.with_name, importlib, shell sources, embedded programs and JS imports. No lexical-reference-only closure claim. |
| Cognito mixed proof | Two provisioner script.py files load Cognito observation. Shared recovery-test.py fixture_root copies it and test_cognito_partial_absent_unknown_and_present_never_mutate loads both controllers; repository contract tests/catalogue also name it. Move/rebind that case with the staging packet or explicitly retain a mixed dependency. No claim that physical isolation alone removes verification coupling. |
| Shared package/test bodies | package-custody-receipt/qualification-test.py still exercises simulated staging builders/workflows. Entity credential-route-test.py explicitly calls restore_fixture.run; other routes import whole test bodies. P5 measures/separates actual consumed responsibilities before promising independent fast proof. |
| Active staging versus history | reconcile-platform-shell-staging/script.py dispatches runtime-alignment/service-update/service-rollback/service-operation-status to release_control.py; seven older pre-change-set modes remain reachable. Keep current transactions, historical modes and release_evidence.py source/receipt readers together during path isolation. |
| Schedules | Preserve reconcile minute 15, synthetic minute 17 and metrics minute 35 every four hours; preserve RAG job's false guard. No trigger, credential or cloud-authority change. |
| RAG embedded sources | Dormant generated recognition is narrowly historical in the migration checker; embedded source lists in retained RAG builders/validators are not universally exempt. Reconcile source strings or an approved historical-source route, without executing corpus regeneration. |
| Pinned recovery / request hashes | `lifecycle/40-package/smoke-test-core-server-persistence-image/qualify-local-public-images.sh` pins exact source bytes; controller/template hashes in original operation reservations must retain their evidence meaning (D-13/U07). Inspect actual owner readers and requalification before moving affected inputs; never edit trust tables merely to manufacture acceptance or replay operations. |
| Historical Entity readers | source-transition.py validates old custody and task-steps.py reads it; its CLI refuses new operations. task-route.py still loads image-delivery-repair history. Preserve historical Git paths and immutable proof bytes; relocate today's reader and actual imports together. A filename is not deletion authority. |
| Checker coverage | Existing path checker exempts commit history, retirement records, dormant generated recognition and narrow accepted proof JSON. Old audit/plan prose elsewhere may still count active. Reconcile that documentation at the actual migration owner; no blanket --allow-active-old-path. Per-path planner/checker runs are deferred until destinations are approved, not claimed passed. |

### Approval boundary and assurance

The concrete legacy homes, E01 layout and finite 173-source scope are approved;
reconcile the placement owner before P6 moves. Each S directory is a
separate bounded packet, not a bulk 95-file move. Preserve the 517 unmoved
sources and all immutable evidence. P5 starts only after the P4 briefing and
explicit approval; no independent source migration authority follows.

One-off in-memory checks reconciled all 901 path mappings, ledger/source equality,
76 Entity members, 173 source moves, unique destinations and no pre-existing
destination. Neither catalogue regeneration nor topology heuristics supplied
semantic approval. Other documentation proof is recorded in the programme.
**P4 is approved at the audit/design boundary. P5 is now authorised; P6 source
movement still requires its milestone start and recorded prerequisites.**


## P5 bounded build/package handoff — 2026-11-25

The user approved proceeding after P4's detailed folder/count briefing. Its
173-source layout is approved, but P6 moves and placement-owner reconciliation
have not started. Source changes below preserve all 690 paths and leaf counts;
P4's source-content digest describes its pinned 1ed9157c snapshot, not these
subsequent dirty bytes. No commit/merge/push or actual publication is performed.

### Before/after call and trust boundaries

| Boundary | Before | P5 change / preserved decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hosted source qualification | Existing infrastructure, full composition and secret-declaration checks requested explicitly. | Same required identities and full task remain; task path/preparation are explicit before execution. No check is disabled. |
| Entity standalone build | Direct catalogue and npm feature commands bypassed saved runner proof; separate composition preparation then default wrapper requested existing members. Existing composition caching may already avoid its repeat execution; no claim it always ran twice. | One existing selector invocation requests diagnostic-catalog-readers, entity-smoke-runtime and entity-smoke-composition, recording preparation and executing only absent/stale prerequisites. Existing aggregate still includes infrastructure and full suite. Dirty source still refuses; Docker arguments and finished-image qualification remain distinct. |
| Qualification capture | Only report field local-runner accepted; genuine trusted-cache result rejected. Uploaded JSON fields alone formed the source qualification record. | CLI computes its own current selector plan without executing tests. Required report identities must match current input/evidence keys and same-worktree private successful receipts. Reused proof is allowed, forged/missing/stale/failed/source-mismatched evidence refuses output. Existing local-qualification/v1 and custody/v2 fields unchanged. |
| Package custody and target read | Existing writer validates source, outcomes and receipt shape; target binding requires the fixed mandatory check set. | Existing generated qualification now passes through actual custody writer into actual target reader in the owned fixture; wrong source refuses a receipt. Image ID/configuration substitution remains the existing qualification.py controlled CLI test's responsibility. No uploaded JSON becomes portable trusted runner proof. |
| Persistence build identity | Builder read HEAD twice and the fixture accepted unequal image-label/build-argument values. | Capture one revision once; strict guard requires both uses in the final build call. Existing actual-builder fixture rejects a second source read and arbitrary substitution. Shared build helper remains unchanged. |
| Pinned recovery | Public-image recovery allowlist names exact original builder bytes. | Rebind only this reviewed source after exact-argv/fail-stop proof; the existing 47-case recovery fixture passes with zero Docker calls and changed-source refusals retained. No wildcard pin relaxation. |

This changes internal source-prerequisite failure projection: the selector
reports the failing registered identity and safe result instead of three
ad-hoc parent source-check wrappers. Fail-stop/nonzero propagation remains,
and no Docker build follows a failed source selection. Existing image-build
provider failure diagnostics/redaction remain at their original wrapper.
No new runtime diagnostic schema, image/custody schema, command layer, source
file, scheduler, test program or execution engine was created. Existing
verification programs were extended; two previously uncalled handoff tests
are now actually reached by the existing publication regression owner.

### Cost evidence and explicit limits

Exploratory connected fixture: three real fixture commands on cold execution,
zero on warm reuse; measured cold 0.100163 seconds / warm 0.033686 seconds.
These are synthetic local checks executed by the real selector, not timings
for the application's full suite. Existing controlled publication/builder
checks passed; public recovery 47 cases passed, zero real Docker calls, stored
configuration unchanged and temporary cleanup confirmed. Final registered
owner results are recorded in the programme, not invented here.

No new parallelism was enabled. Existing payload tests can rename workspace
links and the composition full-suite has its existing parallel route processes;
these were not broadened or certified isolated. Cold dependency setup, full
application/image qualification and end-to-end release timing are **not
established** by controlled fixtures. The prior 21m33s promotion cost remains
a measured problem, not a result erased by local reuse proof.

Runtime scanning was traced, not cached or weakened: the shared
release-succession/ordinary-update.py has release_boundary calls at planning,
authorize and check_authority; the latter remains the immediately-before-action
source/history/current-predecessor check. Three source call sites are not an
observed operation count. Without an authorised live operation, its actual
frequency/timing remains unmeasured. Keep that freshness boundary and original
reservations unchanged; no speculative long-lived runtime cache follows.
Documentation-triggered fan-out (P4 preview's 11 pending instances) is also
retained evidence, not silently reclassified during this bounded handoff fix.
